S2-SO adds `stage2_span_overlap` (WARN, one per pair of sections): a stage-2 record entry (paragraph, table row or table) that shares two or more source elements with an earlier one. Paragraph spans compare by index, table-row spans by row key ("112.3"), and the two never meet, so a whole-table entry is not matched against its own rows. 61 findings on 19 of 278 runs; one run (GLC5JG, a Mocco CV whose entries were read a second time under "Administrative Appointments") holds 19. All 14 findings and 12 pairs read hold the same source lines in two entries, 9 of the 14 across two sections (ATUVAL 868 under HONORS and a committee heading; 2007_Frank_Lau's licence under Certification and under Licensure; web228's publications under Books and Non-refereed), the rest one section's sliding windows (ATUVAL 598/600/602 and 749/752/754, web164 63/65, 2013_Petersen 8/9, web172 112.0-112.2/112.1-112.13/112.3-112.33). Whether stage 6 then prints both copies is not measured: none of the hits was checked against a render, so a true hit here is a stage-2 cut that holds a line twice. A pair sharing one element is not judged: 6,886 such pairs on the same runs are almost all one single-element entry emitted twice, which older stage-2 runs wrote on 42 runs; ATUVAL 863-867/867-869 is one of the 298 that are not, and it is not reported. Most of the within-section windows predate #1798, which merges them in stage 2.

S2-SO2 reports one-element overlaps. A pair of record entries that share one source element is now reported when the two have different spans and their texts share a line, cell or element (`_shares_text`). Before, a pair had to share two. Of the 324 artifacts, 6,886 pairs on 21 share one element with the same span (one element emitted twice); they stay unjudged. 306 pairs on 28 share one element with different spans, and 305 of them share text. The one that does not is a known false positive of a span-only rule, and is no longer reported: web30 1012.0-1012.1 holds only row 0's text (a pre-#1126 span wider than its text) and 1012.1 holds row 1, so row 1 is shared by span alone. Findings go from 61 on 19 artifacts to 112 on 34, and pairs from 344 to 608. At pair level no base pair is lost: every pair the base reports, the branch reports with the same entries and count, because the earlier entry named is still the first that shares two or more elements. At finding level, 34 of the 61 base findings are unchanged and 27 change. None changes its lead entry, and none loses an evidence line: a finding's multi-element pairs are listed first, so its message names the entry it named before and its first evidence lines are the base's. All 27 report a higher count, and 14 of them, which quoted fewer than 3 lines, also gain one-element lines after the base's. (Before the evidence was ordered, 28 changed, 13 led by a different entry, and 18 lost a multi-element evidence line past the 3-line cap, CFGKNQ 1825-1837's 13-element pair among them.) 264 pairs are added: 251 across two sections (most from stage-1b leaf ranges that #916's fixes have since removed: web26, web228, GLC5JG, A5IZ6Q) and 13 within one (IEUPKK 443/444, which #1798 now merges in stage 2). Each was checked by script against the source. For 246, the shared element read from the source docx is in both entries' text. For 18, the local docx is not the one the run read (its element indices differ: FEVLXA, web204, web172), and the shared line is one tab-separated line of both entries' text. Some shared lines are short: a role ("Director") or a year range ("2018-2019") on pre-#1849 artifacts. The ATUVAL autopsy's 863 record is now named, in the evidence of the finding led by entry 867. The 115 replayed outputs (origin/dev's stage 1b and stage 2, LLM replies served from the runs' prompt logs) give 0 findings in both arms, so on current code the change only reports overlaps a later run produces. Worst per-run time is 12 ms. Whether stage 6 prints both copies is still not measured: on the 24 changed runs that have a local (stale) render, the review copy gains 30 comments, no base comment moves, and 6 paragraphs now carry two span-overlap comments, where two findings' quoted lines land on one output paragraph.

JNJ-1 adds `journal_name_joined`: a Q4C/Q4D `journal_name` that holds a tab, a role or section word ("Editorial Board", "Advisory Board", "Ad hoc", "Reviewer", "section", "panel"; "Section" before a capital letter or a numeral is part of a title, as in "Acta Crystallographica Section E"), or two journals joined by a semicolon, a run of two spaces, or a comma between two title-cased runs of two or more words. Each such name renders as one journal row. A comma is read as a join only after the rules that keep one title whole: a one-word piece ("..., Biology, Physics", "..., USA", "JACC, ..."), a last piece after one title that opens with "The", names a publisher ("Hindawi Publishing", "Elsevier") or is a short subject list ("Journal of Neurology, Neurosurgery and Psychiatry"), and an "Official Journal of ..." subtitle. After two title-cased runs the name is a list whatever its last piece is.

241 findings on 72 files (68 uids; re-run after the "Section E" exception, the same 241 findings), one per entry and one offending name each; 237 distinct uid and name pairs (re-runs of one CV, such as IEUPKK, KUUKNJ, TVQZIP, RBHRFR and ZCTARO, repeat). Specialty Consultant Panel" (twice), a panel seat the name keeps.

GCD-1 adds `group_credit_dropped`: a publication whose source byline credits a group, consortium or study team, and whose own rendered bibliography line does not hold every word of that credit. The byline is the source text before stage 4's title (an entry whose source does not hold the title's first three words, or whose byline holds a year, is not judged), split at `,;:.` after removing parentheses and Word `HYPERLINK` field codes; a piece is a credit when it holds a collective word (group, team, investigators, consortium, network, collaborators, collaborative, collaboration, board, committee, task force, alliance) and has at most 8 words, read from after its last "and the", "on behalf of", "for the" or "et al.". The line is searched whole, so a PubMed rebuild that moves the group after the title ("The ACTG 184 Protocol Team.") passes. A citation that also lost the owner is `owner_missing_from_citation`'s, as for `etal_added`. WARN when neither stage 4's authors nor the source names the CV owner, so the group was the owner's only credit (AOJEQD's shape); INFO when the line still names the owner.

Of 39,204 rendered citations on the 303 runs, 644 have a group credit in their byline, and the lint fires on 402 of them, on 42 runs: 161 WARN (18 runs) and 241 INFO. Re-run with origin/dev `32a86bf4` merged in, the 402 findings are the same, message for message. Hand-checked, 20 / 20 WARN and 20 / 20 INFO are true: the source byline credits the group and the rendered line leaves it out, or garbles it into PubMed's inverted initials ("TODAY Study Group" as "Group TS.", "TASMAN Collaborative" as "Collaborative T.", "BARI 2D Study Group (A. Mooradian)" as "Mooradian) B2SG("). In every WARN sampled, nothing left in the line credits the owner. Three shapes the first draft fired on are excluded and are not in the count: a talk led by its date ("December 3, 2011 Baltimore, MD Special Interest Group titled ..."), a stage-4 entry holding several citations (a byline with a year in it), and a pasted link's field code read as a credit.

Recall on labelled runs: all 10 labelled entries of the NECRGC autopsy's group shape are WARN hits (AOJEQD 928, 944, 1004, 1154, 1168, 1170, 1176, 1186 and 1202; QVAQGQ 221). The 181 hits on the 40 NECRGC/JAFPFP runs (61 WARN) are mostly group drops the autopsy did not label: it listed the 9 AOJEQD citations that never name the owner, and AOJEQD has 38 hits. JEFZWH-01's 36 citations ("<group> Investigators (<owner>)") stay `owner_missing_from_citation`'s, so this lint is silent on them. One more hit matches a label by index for another reason: UAURFU 557, where UAURFU-08 records two dropped co-authors and the lint reports the dropped "LIFE Investigators". Not reached: UAURFU-08's longer CV byline with no group credit (790), and a group the source writes with no collective word. All of it is in-sample: the rules were written from these runs.

INST-1 adds `institution_cleaned_name`, #1257's doctor half: an institution name stage 5b's lookup gave (`cleaned_name`, else `official_name`) that loses the CV's own text in the document. It reads the stage-5b JSON and the rendered rows, so it sees the document as stage 6 left it: since #1516, stage 6 puts back a qualifier `cleaned_name` dropped when `cleaned_name` is a substring of the raw value, and the lint stays quiet on those rows (the pilot TXTATQ, WIANVH and JFBPNC rows #1257 cites, and YYVHNN 31, now render the qualifier). A row is the entry's when it shows every word of the name; when several do, only the rows sharing the most years with the entry's text (a "07/21" date cell read as 2021) count, and the qualifier must be missing from all of them. Four shapes, each named in the message:

- `qualifier_dropped`: the name drops words of stage 4's `institution` that are not 5b's city, state or country (`stage6/normalization/institutions.py` `words_beyond_location`, the predicate stage 6 restores by), not a street address, and not a shortening, acronym or corrected spelling of the name's own words ("Dept.", "HSC", "Calrian") or a kind word renamed ("School" for the official "College").
- `source_place_replaced`: the same, where every lost word is a city the CV writes before its state or country; the row prints 5b's place instead.
- `placeholder_name`: the name is "Unknown" (or another placeholder) and the entry's row prints it.
- `address_in_cell`: the CV's institution carries a street address the name dropped, and the row prints it anyway, because stage 6's restore reads a street as a qualifier.

In sample (124 runs): 26 findings on 11 runs, every one read against the entry's source text and the rendered row: 23 true, 3 partial, 0 false.

- `qualifier_dropped` (11): true are CMTQDR 37, 65 and 76 (each department's "Primary academic appointment", "Joint appointment" and "Member" lost), the Douglas-Bell brain bank's "(neurodegenerative diseases)" on four runs of one CV (IEUPKK, KUUKNJ, RBHRFR, ZCTARO 103), RBHRFR 69 (the first of two hospitals dropped) and VYNARH 66 (fifteen hospitals rendered with no town each). Partial: GINAAP and JJUQDF 24 (one CV), where stage 2 cut the line at "Duke Smoking" and 5b rendered "Duke University".
- `source_place_replaced` (7): true are HJPBEM 39, 42, 47, 95 and 100 (the CV's "Palo Alto, CA" rendered "Stanford, CA") and UYQRUN 35 ("West Lafayette" rendered "Indianapolis"). Partial: RNKYST 23, a West Haven hospital the CV places in New Haven, which renders West Haven: the place is right for the hospital and the CV's city is lost.
- `placeholder_name` (8): UYQRUN 600-630, the eight B2 rows "Unknown, <city>" the NDMRSO autopsy named (its ND11). All true.

Held out (53 runs): 6 findings on 5 runs, all true. `qualifier_dropped`: IZIXVF 13 ("MRC Cell Biophysics Unit" and "University of London" lost) and TVQZIP 103 (the brain bank again). `source_place_replaced`: VECUUM 249 (a three-hospital residency rendered under one city, OIEPQD's VECUUM row on #1257) and XVSQFF 6 ("Orange, CA" lost; the row shows no place). `address_in_cell`: SXPHOG 56 and 64, the Manchester rows of YUYVIG SXPHOG-17. The labels carry no entry index for SXPHOG-17, so it is matched by reading, not by index.

Overall 29 of 32 true (91%), 3 partial, 0 false. The shapes were written from the in-sample runs; the held-out 6 of 6 is the first out-of-sample check.

Not caught, and why: a 5b city where the source names none (YUYVIG DYLJXC-02, the generic-hospital residencies): nothing is dropped, and #1791 now withholds those in stage 5b. A name 5b expanded wrongly (ECXGAT-10, "LUMC" read as Leiden): the lint reads words lost, not words added. A doubled location (SPINJC-11, LTTYWI-17): stage 6's location join, not the name. A fan-out child that keeps its parent's name (XELRLZ 23): the name is right for the first record. CULCSV entry 7 (two hospitals in two cities) renders both cities on the current code. A D3 `organization` with a street address and no 5b lookup (SXPHOG Meijer rows, IEUPKK 362): no stage-5b name to compare.

| RRR-1 | 2026-10-16 | `fix/534-renderer-row-receipts` on origin/dev `1c388a65` (`lint_renderer_row_receipts` over each run's render-warnings sidecar, from a fresh `scripts/render_gate.py` render of the branch with no source document) | 166 runs: the 126-run wave-1 farm (`~/worktrees/wave1-corpus/farm`, 82 to 110 distinct CVs) and the 40 NECRGC runs (`~/worktrees/cviche-autopsy-necrgc/analysis`, 40 distinct) | the NECRGC autopsy's two #534 rows (GNONHA Q1, CKDDEG H 90) | #534 |

RRR-1 adds `renderer_row_missing`, #534's doctor half. Stage 6 now keeps a row receipt (`stage6/row_receipts.py`): each row or paragraph a section writes is recorded against the entry it renders, and an entry counts as written only when a row a body section wrote for it is still in the document when the render ends. The lint reads the receipts in the sidecar and warns, once per taxonomy code, about each entry handed to a section that nothing in the document renders and that was not set aside on purpose. A dedup decision excuses no entry: stage 6 dedups before it snapshots what each section is given, so a dropped twin is never on a receipt, and a lost entry that matches a decision's dropped text is the twin dedup kept. The message opens "taxonomy code <code>:", so the run page and the review copy name the section, and the review copy flags that section's heading. Only the sections whose every write path records a receipt are linted: Honors, Memberships, Mentoring and Service (Q1 to Q4D). The other sections still write their receipt to the sidecar, marked `linted: false`.

On the 166 runs, the four linted sections were given 10,215 entries. The lint fires once: CKDDEG, Honors, H 90 ("2014 Earl Benditt Award, North American Vascular Biology Organization"). It is true, checked against the docx's raw `document.xml` (with `w:ins`): no Honors row holds the award, and the only other mention is a different record, an R talk titled "Earl Benditt Award Lecture". The cause is #1930: the honors header check reads the space-separated line as a column-header row. So the lint is 1 / 1 true, and it catches the autopsy's CKDDEG row. GNONHA's Q1 wipe (#454) is fixed on dev, so it no longer fires on the stored GNONHA run. Its shape is pinned by `test_534_a_table_cleared_after_it_was_filled_is_reported_lost`, which bypasses the #454 backstop and sees the lint fire.

A lost entry is matched by identity or by `element_idx_start`: an int, or for a table row the string stage 2 writes ("96.3"). Before the string form was read, 58 Service entries on 16 runs (Opresko, Bostwick and web207 table rows that `_route_q2_entries` copies) read as lost. They render; the copy carries the source's index.

| CUT-1 | 2026-10-19 | `feat/1588-split-record` on origin/dev `99c23548`: `run_doctor` per run, against a worktree of `99c23548`, both arms over the same stored delivered artifacts; and `lint_record_cut_in_two` alone over held-out prod stage-4 JSON | 197 labelled stored runs (as SLC-A3); and 219 prod runs from S3 with stage-4 output, none labelled and none read while the rule was designed (the design read 299 uids) | the local ground-truth labels: 1,683 indexed findings; 61 tied to #1226, E5 or s7ab-9, 28 of which no lint matched at `f4411540` | #1588, #1226 |

CUT-1 adds `record_cut_in_two` (WARN, reads stage 4): one record stage 2 cut into two neighbouring entries of a list. Either the two entries hold complementary parts of the fields the list's other records carry (together all but at most one, sharing at most one), or the second is a description sentence split off the record before it and renders as a record of its own. It skips what `grant_boundary` and `record_boundary` already name. The rule came from reading the 27 of those 28 that origin/dev `a4e56c9b` still missed (`source_line_coverage` now matches the 28th): 8 were already named at another index, and of the 19 left, this shape covers 6 (IZABPD-04, JFGZFT-02, VYRDHN-05, WWSEWY-03a, XYXIQJ-04, XYXIQJ-05). Date pairing across neighbours, a boundary inside one paragraph, and a cut one line early are not judged.

- **Held out, judged blind:** 23 hits on 15 of the 219 prod runs. Each was read by two readers against the source document, mixed with 11 unflagged adjacent pairs from the same lists and not told which was which. They agreed on 32 of 34 items, and a third reader settled the other 2. Verdicts: 17 one record split, 4 two separate records, 2 another defect. In both of the latter, the flagged entry is a real stage-2 fragment, but its other half sits with the other neighbour, so the message names the wrong partner. Precision is 17 / 23 = 0.74 (Wilson 0.54 to 0.87), or 19 / 23 counting a real fragment with the wrong partner. None of the 11 controls was read as a split. The bar was set before the build: WARN at 0.70 or more with a lower bound of at least 0.50.
- **Labelled runs:** 17 hits on 13 of the 197 runs, all WARN. 13 of the 17 land on a labelled defect. The six labels above are now caught, so labels matched by any lint go from 937 to 943 of 1,683, with none lost. Every other lint's findings are identical on 197 of 197 runs.
- **The false positives (4):** separate records whose fields happen to complement each other. Examples: a dated service item beside one that names a committee and a role; a numbered item holding only a title and a protocol number before a full one; a top-level bullet whose sub-bullets stage 2 grouped correctly; a dated talk beside a separate activity paragraph.

| MFI-1 | 2026-10-19 | `fix/1941-xaxvyb` on origin/dev `99c23548` (`lint_mentee_field_incomplete` over each run's stored delivered docx: table rows and body blocks) | 465 S3 runs with a stored `<uid>_wcm.docx` (`s3://wcm-cviche-storage/cviche/runs/`, synced 2026-10-19), 300 distinct CVs by stage-4 `cv_owner` | XAXVYB's corrected copy, comment 25 (every past mentee's Current Position N/A) | #1941 |

MFI-1 adds `mentee_field_incomplete`. It reads each rendered mentee table (the six labels `stage6/sections/mentoring.py` writes) and the Current/Past Mentees heading above it, and reports a row the WCM template asks for that is blank or a placeholder ("N/A", "none", a dash): Site/Position, Mentoring Period and Type of Supervision in every table, Current Position under Past Mentees only. Project/Accomplishments is optional in the template, and a table whose name is blank or "N/A" is the submitter's "N/A" for an empty subsection, which WCM asks for, so it is skipped. One INFO finding per heading and row, naming the first table; it is a review-copy comment on that cell, never a run-page row.

Over the 465 runs it fires on 203, 151 of the 300 distinct CVs, 1 to 7 findings per CV (721 in all: Type of Supervision 301, Current Position 181, Mentoring Period 125, Site/Position 114). XAXVYB fires once, on its 29 past mentees' Current Position. A first draft also read tables named "N/A"; the hand-check found 3 such placeholder tables (two runs) and the name rule now skips them. Hand-checked 40 findings after that, 10 per row, one run per CV, against the docx cell and the stage-4 entry: all 40 are a required cell left blank. In 37 the CV gives no value. In 3 the CV gives one that the stored render lost (a site, a period, a supervision type); two of those render on origin/dev, the stored docx being from older builds. The comment's ask, to fill the field, holds either way, so all 40 count as true.

| PHE-1 | 2026-10-19 | `feat/1949-pinned-heading-empty-lint` on origin/dev `a4e56c9b` (`lint_pinned_heading_empty` alone, per stored stage-3b artifact, with the stage-3a mapping beside it or, failing that, one for the same uid) | 418 distinct uids (485 distinct classified artifacts) found under the main checkout, `~/worktrees/*` and local scratch: every one that could be paired with a stage-3a mapping. 409 more had no 3a anywhere | none: hand-checked | #1949 |

PHE-1 adds `pinned_heading_empty`, #1949's detector for XAXVYB's empty N1 slot (#1938): a heading stage 3a mapped to code X at the header-pin floor (0.95), whose records stage 3b coded outside X's family, while no entry in the CV is X or a child of X. Only the entry's own heading counts (its deepest, up to the three levels stage 3b reads), and a T note is not a record.

As the issue first worded it (no entry under the heading coded X), the lint fired 552 times on 199 of the 418 runs. Most were within a family: a grant under a "Current" heading coded M2B (46), a publication-type call (S4 for S3), K1 for K2, or a heading holding only T notes. The family rule and the T rule bring it to 205 on 84 runs; requiring X to be absent from the whole CV brings it to 59 on 32.

Hand-checked, all 59: 14 true, 7 partial, 38 false. True: both XAXVYB headings (Leadership and mentoring in programs, O for N1, the positive control; Clinical Leadership, O for L3), NSUJZG's six (pipe-split grant rows coded S8, clinical responsibilities coded P, mentoring coded K2 and K3, research associate posts coded B1), BIITRL's administrative teaching leadership coded O, CHXRBM's hospital appointments coded G, GLBCOC's academic leadership roles coded O, JNATFN's industry post coded Q2, web177's clinic service coded K2 and web240's research award coded H. Most of the false ones are correctly coded rows stage 2 placed under the wrong heading: one CV's talks under nine mentorship and service sub-headings (2024), another's under five service headings (2032), mentee lists under a journal-reviewer heading. Those are real defects, but of stage 2's heading context, not of the code. Hence INFO.

| YUY-OAR | 2026-10-27 | `fix/1573-owner-attribution-residual` (`6a31acd5`, and again after its review round, `d588fb7a`), on origin/dev `0daa5392`, against `0daa5392`, each arm's own doctor over its own render of the same day: `scripts/render_gate.py --source-dir` and `scripts/doctor_gate.py` per farm, `--source-dir`; for the stored runs, `run_stage6` from each run's stored stage JSON with LLM calls raising, then `run_doctor` with the run's source docx. A third arm renders the branch over copies of YUYVIG's stored stages 3b to 5d with the stage-3b header-pin replay's recodes applied, keeping stage 4's stored fields (a fresh stage 4 is paid), and doctors it the same way | 126: the 102 of YUY-SPN, and 24 labelled stored runs (YUY-DDR's 23 and RBPXCD); the third arm, YUYVIG's 37 | `_labels_yuyvig_v2` (`scripts/doctor_vs_autopsy.py`); the M1 labels for the farm; the stored runs' labels are not scored here | #1573 |

YUY-OAR measures what #1573's residual fix does to the doctor. In stage 3b, `header_pin.py`'s someone-else's pin recodes to T the rows an acknowledgements, review or staff heading or lead line shows are not the owner's, and an author-led abstract under an abstracts leaf from H to S8. In stage 6, `bibliography.py` prints a disclaiming or edited-volume source heading after each citation under it (` (listed under "<heading>")`), and `service.py` leaves a role the source writes against another named person out of the owner's Role cell. The doctor's one change is `_bibliography_lines` (`lints/render.py`), which removes that note before a line is paired with its source, with `SOURCE_HEADING_NOTE_RE` imported from `bibliography.py`. It reaches every lint that reads `_rendered_citations`. A line without the note is read as before, and no dev-259 render carries one, so `precision.HELD_OUT_CHANGED` is unchanged.

Over the stored stage-3b outputs every report is the same in both arms, on both branch commits: YUYVIG 1,074 findings (WARN 412, INFO 662), the farm 2,630 (ERROR 1, WARN 1,652, INFO 977), the 3 other runs 228 (WARN 41, INFO 187) and the 24 stored runs 901 (WARN 484, INFO 417). `scripts/doctor_gate_compare.py` passes on the three farms. The renders do change: 6 Role cells on DYLJXC, and on the stored runs the note after 49 RBPXCD, 13 GNONHA and 55 LFFKVV citations. The note strip is what keeps the reports equal. Positive control: with dev's `lints/render.py` on the branch tree, RBPXCD loses 9 `group_credit_dropped` WARN and 1 `owner_attribution` INFO, because with the note left in, those citations fall below the pairing share with their sources.

The third arm shows stage 3b's part, which a render of stored stage-3b output cannot. The replay's 72 recodes include 50 on YUYVIG: ECXGAT 26 (22 bibliography rows, S1, S2, S4, S5 and S9, under an acknowledgements lead line, and 4 N3B rows under a review lead line), IZIXVF 22 (a staff leaf), DYLJXC 1 (a thesis under a reviewer lead line) and ULHCAP 1 (H to S8). The other 22 are LRANWT, a stored re-run of IZIXVF's CV. The farm and the 24 stored runs hold none, so their third arm would equal the branch. YUYVIG goes from 1,074 findings to 1,069 (WARN 412 to 409, INFO 662 to 660), on 4 runs:

- `owner_attribution` WARN 4 to 0, its 16 INFO unchanged. `citation_without_owner` on ECXGAT 2156-2204 and `mentee_under_non_mentee_heading` on ECXGAT 1293-1299, IZIXVF 284-305 and DYLJXC 403, all 4 true by YUY-OA's reading, go silent because those rows now print as Appendix lines.
- IZIXVF loses its `taxonomy_code_coverage` INFO (a bare N3 with no route) and the `stage6_render_warnings` INFO for a bare N3 resolved to N3B.
- ULHCAP gains one `stage6_render_warnings` WARN, shape `appendix_section_declined`. Entry 1415, recoded S8 but carrying its stored H fields, has no title, so the bibliography declines it and it prints verbatim in the Appendix. A fresh stage 4 would extract S8 fields and it would render under Abstracts; that is paid and was not run.
- Every other finding that differs on the 4 runs changes only a count, an index or a code in its message or evidence: `output_hygiene`'s Appendix counts, the T-diverted count of `stage6_render_warnings`, `mentee_field_incomplete`'s table counts and examples, `duplicate_records`' block indexes, an evidence index of `junk_or_header_row` on DYLJXC, the code `group_header_context` names on ECXGAT (N3B to T), and `llm_fallback_served`'s entry counts by code, an artifact of stored stage-4 fields under new codes.

Recall: base and branch catch the same label ids on both sets, YUYVIG 94 of 250 (HIGH 18/41, MED 39/108, LOW 37/101) and the farm 252 of 487 (HIGH 49/84). The third arm catches 92 of 250 (HIGH 17, MED 38, LOW 37). It loses ECXGAT-01 (HIGH) and IZIXVF-01 (MED), which only `owner_attribution` caught (IZIXVF-01 also through the stage-6 bare-N3 message): their defects no longer render, so these are fixes, as IZABPD-06 was in YUY-DDR. There `owner_attribution` goes from 20 hits, 3 matched and 3 caught to 16, 1 and 1; OKRTPJ-03 is still caught. ULHCAP's new WARN names no entry index, so it cannot match a label. The per-lint table is unchanged: the 4 WARNs leave because their defects stop rendering, not because a verdict changed. The pin and the stage-6 rules were written from these YUYVIG runs and the stored runs, so all of it is in-sample.

M2-junk added `junk_or_header_row` and changed no other lint's findings on any run. All 104 hits were hand-read against the stage-4 entry and the rendered row: the 39 unmatched are 38 group headers, lead-in labels, date fragments or banner titles printed as records, and 1 partly so (a banner title rendered with a date); of the 65 matched, 64 are and 1 is partly so (a header row that also shows a role). 102 of 104 (98%). The shapes were tuned on this farm, so the next batch is the first out-of-sample check.
M2-enrich added `pubmed_title_truncated` and `enrichment_pubtype_mismatch` and changed no other lint's findings on any run. Every hit was hand-read against the stage-4 title and the accepted text of the base docx (text inside `w:ins` included, `w:delText` excluded). `pubmed_title_truncated`: 22 of 22 are cut titles that render cut, followed directly by the journal; the 14 unmatched sit on 6 runs the autopsies did not record them for. `enrichment_pubtype_mismatch`: 2 of 2 render a correction notice's title where the CV lists the paper; the unmatched one is a CV that gives the notice's PMID for the paper. Both read stage-5 JSON built before #1358, which fixed both causes at the source, so on newer runs they guard against a regression.

No hits on M1's 62 runs: `bucket_status`, `dead_sections`, `invented_records`, `llm_refusal_in_output`, `no_output`, `owner_contact_missing`, `pipeline_errors_present`, `protected_data_in_output`, `python_repr_in_output`, `segmentation`, `stage3b_fallback_ratio`, `stage3b_second_pass_error`, `stage_failure_recorded`, `table_lost`, `under_extraction`, `unrendered_records`.

## Held-out precision (YUY-HO)

One row per lint key that fired on a YUYVIG run, in either doctor. **in-sample** is the per-lint table's TP / judged above (summed over a lint's shapes), with its measurement. **held-out** is the YUYVIG verifiers' WARN verdicts on the dev-259 doctor (INFO findings were not judged). **points** is held-out minus in-sample. A lint is marked **overfit** when its held-out precision is 20 points or more below its in-sample number. **matched / located** and **caught** are `doctor_vs_autopsy.py` on the current doctor against the v2 labels. Hits are over 36 runs for dev-259 and 37 for the current doctor; the held-out verdicts cover all 37 (IXJMKS's verifier judged a local re-run, since the run kept no doctor report).

## Repair tiers (#1591)

What the review copy (`<uid>_wcm_review.docx`) does with each lint or shape's findings, chosen from the precision above. From most to least certain:

- **Certain fix**: the doctor can derive the corrected text from the run's own artifacts, and the shape is right at least 99% of the time on at least 100 judged findings. The fix is written as a Word tracked change: accepting it gives the corrected text, rejecting it gives the delivered text, and either choice is a verdict for #1587.
- **Suggestion**: a fix the doctor can derive, right at least 80% of the time on at least 20 judged findings (the #822 / #813 bar). It is meant to be a tracked change too. None is built yet, so these are comments today.
- **Comment**: a Word comment on the text the finding is about (`review_comments.py`).
- **Review note**: below 50% (`IN_PLACE_MIN_PRECISION`), `precision.shown_in_place` (#1589) keeps the finding off the text. Since #2002 it is not in the copy at all, only on the admin run page's Diagnostics tab; so is a `stage6_render_warnings` finding that no heading comment places, except an Appendix diversion, which is a note (#1639). Two lints are items in the review-notes comment on the copy's first paragraph whatever their row: `source_line_coverage` and `dedup_drops`.
- **Section re-run**: #813, for a lint `precision.remediation_allowed` admits. None does.

**Precision used.** For the fix tiers, the lower of in-sample and held-out, each counted only when it has at least 5 judged findings, and judged findings summed over both. There are two exceptions:

- `span_count` and `year_not_in_source` were held out before #1607 fixed their false positives, so their post-fix in-sample rows (Y1585) stand alone.
- The held-out table pools `role_consistency` and `group_header_context` over their shapes, so their shape rows show that pooled figure.

**Comment versus review note.** These two tiers are the gate's, read from `precision.load_gate_ledger()`: the per-lint row with YUYVIG's held-out verdicts folded in for a lint unchanged since dev-259 (`HELD_OUT_CHANGED` excluded). The **gate (combined)** column gives that figure where it differs from in-sample, and says "same" where it does not, with the reason when a held-out figure exists but is not folded. Moving a lint between these tiers is a ledger change, not a code change. Against the in-sample figure alone, the combined one moves six rows:

- To comments: `classified_unrendered` (1 / 2, 50%), `enrichment_failures` (9 / 15, 60%), and the stage-6 shapes `appendix_recovered` (1 / 2, 50%), `appendix_recovered_A` (18 / 35, 51%) and `reroute_cross_family` (7 / 13, 54%).
- To a review note: `date_cell_shape` (1 / 4, 25%, held-out verdicts only).

`appendix_no_route_T` stays a review note at 15 / 34 (44%). `duplicate_records` stays a comment at 11 / 20 (55%). `owner_missing_from_citation` is a comment at 28 / 35 (80%), which the M3 cap table's in-sample figures bring in.

**Built today:**

- The certain fix for `owner_pi_role_empty`. `review_comments._suggest_owner_pi_role` inserts "PI" in the empty "Your role:" cell of every grant table the lint reports (`doctor/lints/extraction.owner_pi_role_empty_tables`), and a finding fixed on every table it reports gets no comment. A reported table with no "Your role:" row is left as delivered, and its finding stays a comment.
- Separately, `protected_data_in_output` is auto-applied upstream: stage 6's #1389 repair (`CVICHE_RUN_REPAIR=1`) withholds the value and records it in `<uid>_repairs.json`.

**Not built:**

- The certain fix for `etal_added`.
- Every suggestion row.
- Applying certain fixes to the delivered `<uid>_wcm.docx`; they reach the review copy only.

Any lint not listed here has no verdicts and is a comment.

## Score cap inputs (M3)

#1595 (2026-10-08) retired the content-loss caps this section qualified: each lint now lowers the score by its findings' precision-weighted fix minutes instead of capping it (`quality_score.score_doctor_findings`, docs/RUN_DOCTOR_SCORING.md). The rows below are kept as the measurements those caps were set on.

The quality score caps a run at 84 on more lints only while this table records the cap's own findings at 80% precision or more on 20 or more of them (Paul's decision on #822, 2026-10-02). Each row measures the subset of the lint's findings that would cap, not the whole lint. **matched** is M1's index match; every unmatched hit was hand-checked against the rendered docx (`w:ins` text included) and the stage-4 entry. A partial is a real defect other than the one the cap claims, and counts against precision.

| cap input | caps when | hits | matched | hand-checked: TP / partial / FP | precision | caps? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `owner_missing_from_citation`, the hits on runs with 3 or more (XWNZWW 10, BMHBJZ 6, BMAMWE 4, NJIKGI 3) | 3 or more on a run | 23 | 11 | 12 / 0 / 0 of 12 | 23 / 23 (100%) | yes |
| `owner_missing_from_citation`, lint-wide (for reference; 6 of these hits sit on runs with 1 hit and never cap) | | 29 | 13 | 14 / 1 / 1 of 16 | 27 / 29 (93%) | |
| `multi_record_coverage`, WARN with 2 or more clauses or values on no line | 1 or more | 20 | 14, of which 3 are `field_lost`, hand-checked: 1 TP, 2 partial | 2 / 1 / 3 of 6 | 14 / 20 (70%) | no |
| `offschema_fields`, record-shaped WARN | 1 or more | 8 | 6 | 1 / 0 / 1 of 2 (the other was rendered fused into raw text) | 7 / 8, n under 20 | no |
| `dedup_drops` | 1 or more lossy | 13 (6 WARN), 17 drops | names text | of the 17 drops, 7 have their text on one rendered line at 0.9 or more of its words; EBYSBC verdicts 2 / 2 / 4 of 8 | n under 20 | no |

`owner_missing_from_citation`'s partial is a data-safety-board credit dropped from a trial citation, and its false positive a co-presented talk whose rendered line names only the other presenter. Both sit on runs with a single hit, so neither caps. Its 13 matched hits sit on verified findings of the owner-dropped classes (EBYSBC E13 and E32, and s7ab-8); 11 of them are on the capping runs. The unmatched 14 true positives are owner credits the autopsies did not list one by one: eight cut by "et al.", and six study-group or collaborator credits ("including ...") the rendered line leaves out. The doctor reports 30 hits; the 30th is on QFJSXR, the one run with no autopsy labels, so the harness does not score it, and with 1 hit it never caps.

`multi_record_coverage`'s false positives are one record's own detail split across two source lines (a meeting's name and its venue, twice on one run) and a mentee list the docx renders on one line; its partials lose a mentee's career detail, one of two committee roles, or mentees' years while their names render.

### Zero-false-positive WARN lints (M4-cap, 2026-10-05)

The RCBKFG autopsy (6 runs, dev-247) found 0 false positives for `grant_boundary`, `grant_bucket`, `junk_or_header_row` and `etal_added`, and Paul approved feeding them into the cap on 2026-10-05. Each row pools that batch with the ledger rows above and with the NDMRSO autopsy (30 runs, dev-246), whose per-run `doctor_review` judged these lints on the dev-HEAD doctor; NDMRSO is the first batch these shapes were not written from. Hits are counted with origin/dev `10f18e34`'s lints over each run's stored stage-4 JSON and docx (`analysis/<uid>/`), and over the EBYSBC farm's base render of `fb466a0f`. A hit "matched" names an entry a verified autopsy finding lists; an unmatched hit was read by hand. A partial counts against precision.

| cap input | caps when | lint-wide TP / judged | cap subset: TP / judged | caps? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `grant_boundary` | 3 or more on a run | 68 / 70 (97%): farm 44 / 45 (W3B-GR), RCBKFG 14 / 14, NDMRSO 10 / 11 | 59 / 59: ZCTARO 13, CXRYCF 10, CTWLTR 8, VGHNZD 5 (farm); KUUKNJ 13 (RCBKFG N1); DXAGUS 6 (DXAGUS-01/-02), VYRDHN 4 (VYRDHN-02) | yes |
| `grant_bucket`, an application rendered as an award | 1 or more | 30 / 30 (100%): ZDCXIV 15 (ZDCXIV-01), FLYBMX 15 (RCBKFG); one CV in two runs | same 30 / 30 | yes |
| `grant_bucket`, a Current grant whose end date has passed | | 2 / 2: KYOPUV 589 (farm), CAOACN 589 (RCBKFG) | n under 20, and the shape reads today's date | no |
| `junk_or_header_row` | 5 or more on a run | 151 / 165 (92%): farm 102 / 104 (M2-junk), RCBKFG 9 / 9, NDMRSO 40 / 52 (77%; 38 / 52 before JUNK-EV) | 102 / 105 (97%): farm 69 hits on 6 runs, the 2 M2-junk partials counted as if they sit there; NDMRSO KHXOUF 14 / 15 (12 / 15 before JUNK-EV), BNYLDF 11 / 11, SYWZJA 5 / 5, GCFEBE 5 / 5 | yes |
| `etal_added` (WARN since X6-cite) | 3 or more on a run | RCBKFG 92 / 92; NDMRSO 17 judged lines, 0 false; X6 95 / 95 | 925 / 925 on the 79 runs it moves out of GREEN (54 distinct; X6-cite cap, below) | yes, since 2026-10-07 |

`grant_boundary`'s two false positives (RXYBVF 502, VYNARH 648) sit on runs with 1 and 2 hits, and a lone hit is one grant's edge; a slipped stage-2 cut carries down the list, which is what 3 or more catches. The NDMRSO 10 / 11 is DXAGUS 6 (judged TP) and VYRDHN 4 (each names a VYRDHN-02 record), against VYNARH 648.

X6-grant (2026-10-06) re-read RXYBVF 502 as true (see its measurement), leaving VYNARH 648 the one false positive, on a run with 2 hits. Its line-order shape moves six runs over the cap's 3, all true: RVTAQT 7 (RVTAQT-01) and its re-runs AQCLHS, GINAAP and JJUQDF 7 each, RXYBVF 9 (RXYBVF-02/-03), and web205 5 (1 before; the 4 new hits are two split grants, and the old one, 648, is a description-and-role tail of the grant before it). That makes the cap subset 59 + 42 = 101 of 101. The first measurement missed web205 and gave 96 of 96. The cap gate is unchanged: it already counts every `grant_boundary` finding.

`junk_or_header_row` out of sample is the weakest number here: NDMRSO judged 22 TP, 3 partial and 11 FP over its reviewed runs (EHGXAL 3 of 3 false: trainee headings that do group their awards; UYQRUN 2 of 3 quote a row that is not the entry's own, KHXOUF 3 of 15 quote another row or name an entry stage 6 dropped, of which JUNK-EV fixes the 2 that quoted another row (1252, 1261); REOYVH and SVYSGY 1 of 1). BNYLDF's 11 and GCFEBE's 5 were not in that review and were read for this row: BNYLDF 9 are BNYLDF-03 and 2 are military-award lead-in labels printed as rows; GCFEBE's 5 are GCFEBE-06. Every NDMRSO false positive sits on a run with 1 to 3 hits, so the cap needs 5.

`etal_added` caps a run at 3 or more hits (Paul, 2026-10-07). What it reports: a rendered citation whose author list stops at "et al." where the source names every author, so co-authors are gone; a citation that also lost the owner is `owner_missing_from_citation`'s finding, not this one's, so the two gates never count one line twice. Until #1404 stage 5d cut every list past six authors, which is why the lint fires on 94 of the 111 stored runs. #1404 is merged and deployed (dev-248 onward): on the 9 EOAHMI runs built on dev-248 (951 paired citations) it fires 0 times, so on a new run a hit is a list 5d cut anyway. Why 3 and not 1: precision does not set it, since no judged hit has been false; 3 is the owner-missing gate's count for the same kind of harm (author credit cut from a citation), and one or two cut lists are a quick fix by hand.

All 925 hits on the 79 runs were read against stage 4's source text and the rendered docx (`w:t` with `w:ins`, `w:delText` dropped): every line is on the page as the lint quotes it, and every source names at least one author the line drops before "et al.". 894 were checked by script (a surname in the source's author list that the line's list lacks; 20 of them re-read by eye) and 31 by hand, where the script could not separate the author list (title first, a surname repeated among editors, a source line cut mid-list): all true. 925 / 925, 0 false. ## Recall (M1)

All lints together matched 60 of the 487 indexed verified findings (12%). By severity: high 22 of 84 (26%), medium 24 of 222 (11%), low 14 of 181 (8%). By batch: EBYSBC 33 of 312, s7ab 18 of 112, pilot 9 of 63. 29 more verified findings, all from the pilot, carry no entry index: their evidence names none, or names entry numbers the conversion could not resolve. No hit can match them.

Matching by index both under- and over-credits, so compare it with the autopsies' own judgement. EBYSBC and the pilot recorded whether the doctor caught each finding: yes for 46, partly for 17, of 404.

Per-class recall (`by_batch_class` and `by_class_ref`) is in the `--json` output. Recompute it rather than copying it here. Where each batch's classes come from:

- EBYSBC: `batch_class` is the synthesis's own class, `E1`..`E36`, and `class_ref` is the cross-batch class each verified finding cites, mostly an s7ab class (`s7ab-1`..`s7ab-21`).
- s7ab: its verified findings carry no class. The label converter reads the class table in the s7ab synthesis, which names each class's records by uid and entry index in prose. A finding gets `s7ab-<n>`, as both `batch_class` and `class_ref`, when exactly one class row names its uid and one of its indices. When several rows do, it gets one only if exactly one of those rows lists the finding's own category. This assigns 83 of the 112. Each of the 83 was read against the finding's own description, and one was wrong: RXYBVF-04 is set by hand to `s7ab-2`, whose row gives that run a record count rather than an index. Of the other 29, 2 are named by more than one row and 27 by none. Some rows give no index at all: class 1, for instance, names its runs and record counts only. Those 29 are grouped as `(none)`, so per-class recall for s7ab covers 83 findings.
- pilot: `class_ref` is the pilot's `failure_class`. It has no batch class.

## Recall (YUY-HO, held-out)

**The doctor caught 19 of 53 HIGH (held-out)** on origin/dev `a7ca2f66`. 13 are the ones the YUYVIG verifiers credited to the dev-259 doctor (5 fully, 8 partly), and each one's lint still fires on its run. The other 6 come from lints merged on 2026-10-08 and written from these runs, so they are in-sample: ECXGAT-01 and ECXGAT-03 (`owner_attribution`), and HXBPCT-01, LTTYWI-01, OKRTPJ-01 and WPJHYT-03 (`dedup_drops` WARN, as #1611 read them). Out of sample, the doctor caught 13 of 53 HIGH.

At any severity it caught 127 of 444 (29%): high 19 of 53, medium 45 of 167, low 63 of 224. Here "caught" means a verifier credit (yes or partial), or a hit of the current doctor on the finding's entry where no dev-259 hit was, or one of the 4 PR readings named above. By index match alone (`doctor_vs_autopsy.py`): the current doctor matches 77 of 250 indexed findings (31%; high 15 of 41, medium 26 of 108, low 36 of 101), and dev-259 matches 73 of 246.

`doctor_vs_autopsy.py --json` gives the index-matched half of this (`by_class_ref`, `by_stage`). The ranking adds the verifiers' credits and the PR readings, using `_yuyvig_autopsy/rank_misses.py` (local).

## How to re-measure

The farm and the labels hold CV content, so they live outside the repo:

- `~/worktrees/eb-farm/` holds the 63 runs' stage outputs (`outputs/`), their source docx (`src_docx/`) and a base render (`base/`).
- `~/worktrees/eb-labels/<uid>.json` holds one label file per run, converted from each run's verified autopsy (`analysis/<uid>/autopsy_verified.json`, and the pilot's `analysis/pilot/_tools/wf_result.json`), with s7ab's classes read from its synthesis (`docs/analysis/AUTOPSY-s7ab-batch-2026-10-02.md`, local). A label holds ids, codes, severities, entry indices and verdicts, and no CV text. The schema is in `scripts/doctor_vs_autopsy.py`'s docstring.

Run from the worktree of the arm being measured. A doctor-only change can reuse the base render; a change to stage 6 renders first (`scripts/render_gate.py`, see `docs/guides/render-doctor-gates.md`) and passes its own render directory.

```bash
FARM=$(mktemp -d)/farm
~/worktrees/eb-farm/mkdocfarm.sh ~/worktrees/eb-farm/base "$FARM"
PYTHONPATH=src python3 scripts/doctor_gate.py "$FARM" "$FARM-doctor.json" --source-dir ~/worktrees/eb-farm/src_docx
python3 scripts/doctor_vs_autopsy.py "$FARM-doctor.json" ~/worktrees/eb-labels --json "$FARM-score.json"
```

M1 skipped the first two steps: it scored the existing `~/worktrees/eb-farm/doctor_base.json`, a `doctor_gate.py` run of `c3d87c5f` over the base farm (397 findings on 63 runs).

The YUYVIG held-out set (YUY-HO) lives under `~/worktrees/cviche-autopsy-b13/analysis/`: one run per `<uid>/` (`outputs/` stage JSON, `input/` source), the v2 labels in `_labels_yuyvig_v2/`, and the converters in `_yuyvig_autopsy/`. Build a flat farm from `<uid>/outputs` (one `stage_*` directory per suffix, as `~/worktrees/eb-farm/outputs`), copy each docx source to `<uid>.docx` in a source directory, then render and doctor it:

```bash
PYTHONPATH=src python3 scripts/render_gate.py "$FARM" "$RENDER" --source-dir "$SRC"
mkdir -p "$FARM/stage_6_wcm_documents" && cp "$RENDER"/*_wcm.docx "$RENDER"/*_render_warnings.json "$FARM/stage_6_wcm_documents/"
PYTHONPATH=src python3 scripts/doctor_gate.py "$FARM" "$OUT/doctor.json" --source-dir "$SRC"
python3 scripts/doctor_vs_autopsy.py "$OUT/doctor.json" ~/worktrees/cviche-autopsy-b13/analysis/_labels_yuyvig_v2 --json "$OUT/score.json"
```

From YUYVIG on, every batch autopsy adds its labels the same way, so the held-out set grows: convert the verified per-run reports into this schema (with `stage`, `class_ref` and the WARN verdicts in `doctor_review`), keep them beside the batch's runs, and add a held-out measurement row here.

`--json` also lists each lint's unmatched hits (uid and entry index) and the runs of its unlocated hits, which is the sample for a hand-check. A lint PR reports its row before and after, hand-checks up to 30 unmatched hits, and adds a measurement id here.
